### Предфильтр страшного сообщения: ИИ сначала проверяет, не плохое ли оно  ·  [02:58](https://youtu.be/NUU1n5O6d4o?t=178)
**Мысль:** Тревога перед открытием письма/сообщения создаёт барьер; если посредник заранее снимает угрозу, человек перестаёт откладывать.

> And another way to pre-filter information is if you're actually scared of the information. If you don't want to know it for some reason, let's say there is a text from your friend just waiting on your phone, and you're not sure if it's good or bad, or you see an email from your boss and you're thinking, "Damn, what if it's bad?" Pre-filtering the information here can be, "Here is this thing that I received. Like, don't look at it. Just copy-paste it. Don't read the actual text. Give it any important context, like, this is from my boss, and if this is bad, it might mess up my confidence and I'll be thinking about it all evening. Please don't tell me what it's about, but in case it's not bad, then tell me what it's about, so then I can reply." I have done that so many times, and most of the time it wasn't anything scary, so it just helped me to overcome that initial blockage of looking at whatever it is, and it saved me the dread of keeping that task in the back of my head for the next month.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник может предлагать «посмотрю сам, скажу мягко, что там» для пугающей задачи — снижение эмоционального барьера старта.


### Застревание часто ложная дилемма: ищем середину, а не силу воли  ·  [03:52](https://youtu.be/NUU1n5O6d4o?t=232)
**Мысль:** Выбор «A или B» обычно ложен; промежуточный вариант, к которому тянет, снимает паралич. Совет «заставь себя» не работает.

> Getting unstuck or overcoming decision fatigue. Like we will often be like, "I want to do this, but I actually have to do this. So therefore, I'll be on my phone or I'll be doing nothing." You will be surprised how often that happens. But as a coach, I've noticed that it's often a logical fallacy. It's not do A or do B, and you can't do that. There are often middle grounds, you know, like the previous one where we talked about removing the emotional barrier first. And what I'll do is I'll just tell the whole scenario to ChatGPT. I'll be like, "Hey, I want to write a script, but I can't because I actually feel like to complete the script, I need to have a better website, which will be linked underneath the video." Literally happened to me this week. And ChatGPT said, "Well, you can write the script draft. And once you have done that, you can go back and do some decisions for the website. They will help you move forward with the next steps, and eventually when that's done, you can add that to finalize your script." And there you go, you're no longer stuck because there is a middle ground. It's not all or nothing. Tell whatever your story is to ChatGPT and ask for some perspective or some options that you can take that are ADHD friendly. Things you are inclined to doing because if it tells you, "Oh, just force yourself to do the thing you don't want to do," that's not going to work.

**Для Напарника:** В разбиении задачи (api/breakdown) предлагать «середину»: начерно один кусок вместо всего-или-ничего; без принуждения.


### Ролевая репетиция сценария снимает тревогу перед реальным делом  ·  [10:47](https://youtu.be/NUU1n5O6d4o?t=647)
**Мысль:** Проигранный в игре сценарий превращается в пережитый опыт, и страх первого раза уменьшается.

> And practice for learning and confidence. Because if you were, let's say, a Renaissance person and you were still alive today, you would definitely ace a history test on the Renaissance because your brain would remember the sounds, the sights, the people, the daily struggles. It wouldn't just feel like some facts on a piece of paper. It would be your real life experience. And obviously, you can't time travel unless you know something that I don't, but you can do something very similar in ChatGPT, which is to play pretend and help yourself learn faster, better, and actually remember things in a way that sticks. Like, I'm learning about this thing. Can we pretend that I'm actually doing it? Give me a character, give me a task. Let's run through the scenario where I have to use my knowledge like I actually would need to in real life. That also removes some of the anxiety about trying something in a real situation because now you've practiced it. Like it gives you the confidence that you would know how to act if it did happen.

**Для Напарника:** Идея для напарника: «давай прорепетируем» трудный разговор/звонок перед запуском задачи.

# 🎬 20 Executive Dysfunction hacks for ADHD #adhd #adhdwomen — Micheline Maalouf


### Найти свои продуктивные часы и не требовать работы в провал  ·  [02:00](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=120)
**Мысль:** Стандартный график 8-5 не подходит всем; попытки работать в личный спад порождают стыд. Нужен эксперимент, чтобы найти окно.

> The next tip if you do have some flexibility or some choices in how you break up your tasks find the time of day that you are most productive we're so different so some people are really productive in the morning some people are more productive in the afternoon but we all have this idea in our minds that we need to wake up first thing in the morning and get work done and just do the eight to five thing and although that's just how the system set it up it doesn't mean it's going to work for you for me I find that I am more productive between 10 a.m to 1pm but I have a major crash between two and three around that time if I do anything nothing's going to get done and I'm going to end up shaming myself for even trying to do something and feeling horrible for not being able to do it so knowing that I have to take a break between two and three is really important and this might take some trial and error so you might have to play a round of it to see when your best Focus time is you may not be aware of it.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин напарника может спрашивать, когда тебе легче всего, и предлагать Фокус в это окно; в провал — мягкий отдых без вины.


### Body doubling: присутствие человека с делом рядом мотивирует  ·  [02:54](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=174)
**Мысль:** Просто сидеть рядом с тем, кто тоже работает, без разговоров, даёт мотивацию делать свои задачи.

> Body doubling is simply getting somebody that also has a lot of work to do to work next to you as a warm body but you're not allowed to like engage and talk with them and like waste time you're really just there as they're working and you're working because something about having somebody next to us while they're doing their tasks feels more motivating for us to do ours.

**Для Напарника:** Кот у костра в Фокусе — именно эта метафора присутствия; подчёркивать «молча рядом, не надзирает».


### Склеить неприятное дело с любимым подкастом: дело становится условием удовольствия  ·  [04:12](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=252)
**Мысль:** Подкаст можно слушать только за уборкой, поэтому дело превращается в способ получить интересное; нелюбимая задача идёт 4 часа.

> Habit stacking has literally saved my life I hate cleaning like I hate organizing not because I don't like a clean space because I function best with a clean space but it also feels like there's too much to do and I get really overwhelmed so I love listening to podcasts specifically like True Crime podcasts or something really interesting about psychology I'll find a podcast ahead of time that I know has a lot of episodes that I can like fly through or want to binge on and I put that in my ears and then I start cleaning and all of a sudden it's like four hours later and I've completed everything I needed to do and it's not because it makes me like to clean but it just makes it so I don't want to stop listening to the podcast and so I want to clean because that's the only way I can also focus on the podcast because if I were to sit down and put the earbuds in I wouldn't be able to pay attention to the podcast or whatever it is I'm listening to.

**Для Напарника:** В запуске задачи напарник может предлагать «заряд» — музыку/подкаст на время шага; звук костра как фон.

**Для Напарника:** В разбиении: «собери всё в одно место» как первый микрошаг для уборки.

**Мысль:** Если нужный предмет далеко, по дороге отвлечёшься на другое; реальный пример с двумя этажами и дублированием швабры.

> Have cleaning products in each room so one thing that would like really suck for me is I have a two-story home so I would want to clean upstairs but I'm like oh the mop is downstairs the vacuum's downstairs the Clorox wipes are downstairs I have to walk down and come up and by the time I went down to do something I already found something else that interested me so things never got done so I went and I bought a separate mop for upstairs a separate box of Clorox wipes for upstairs I'm in the process of finding a new vacuum for upstairs and that way there's no excuses if it's there if it's in front of your face and you don't have to work extra hard to get the cleaning product it's going to be so much easier to just do it.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкие напоминания о еде/воде как «встречи» напарника, без давления.

**Для Напарника:** Награда в Мире/острове как здоровый дофамин; можно предлагать «отдых» как запланированный элемент дня.


### Делать «на отвали» и ограничить список тремя делами  ·  [09:57](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=597)
**Мысль:** Прокрастинация — про чувства неадекватности и перегруза в начале; ожидание плохого результата снижает барьер. Список из 15 пунктов парализует, три — нет.

> This tip actually helped me a lot my psychiatrist gave me this tip half ass everything procrastination is not about anything other than like what you feel when you're about to start a task it's a lot about feeling inadequate feeling overwhelmed trying to avoid those feelings like when we sit down to do something and we get that Rush of oh my God this is so much that's what causes us to procrastinate so I want you to go into the mindset of I'm going to half-ass it this is going to suck I'm not expecting it to be good and just start with the expectation that it will be horrible that's how you get yourself going another thing that really helps is limit your to-do list I bet that if you looked at your to-do list right now you have at least 15 things on there and then you look at it and you're like of course I can't do anything it's so overwhelming I don't want to do any of it sit down take a breath cut it down to three things three things you want to do today.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: старт с «сделаю плохо, и это ок»; Дом: на сегодня максимум три шага, не больше.


### Выговорить все «не могу, потому что» в голосовую заметку и стартовать заново  ·  [11:14](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=674)
**Мысль:** Список отговорок крутится в голове; вынос наружу голосом быстрее дневника и освобождает голову для старта.

> When I'm about to start something I have all these complaints about it like I can't do this because of a b c d e f g and how am I even supposed to get started because I need to do this before I can do this and that's where my mind goes I start thinking of all the reasons why I can't do that thing right now and what's really helpful is to open up your voice notes on your phone and just record all your complaints all the reasons why you can't do it all the reasons that are getting in your way get it out of your system I would tell you to journal but let's be honest as an ADHD or you probably can't keep up with journaling but voice memos are so much easier because they're just faster so get the phone out voice record you don't have to listen to it just get it out of your system and then try to start again and see if that helps you.

**Для Напарника:** В чате напарника: кнопка «выплесни все причины, почему не хочется» перед созданием первого шага.


### Заранее договориться, чтобы критика не читалась как «я плохой»  ·  [13:34](https://youtu.be/Yi1szTIJy4U?t=814)
**Мысль:** Из-за отвержения-чувствительности замечание воспринимается как приговор; договорённость заранее переводит это в рабочий сигнал.

> Rejection sensitivity so if somebody says you interrupted me that might come to you like I'm a bad friend and a lot of Shame and you might feel really horrible about it so a way to avoid that is to have the conversation and come to an agreement beforehand so that you know hey they're not doing this because they think I'm a horrible person but we did come to the agreement that if I interrupt them or if this happens that they're going to help me kind of work through it.

**Для Напарника:** Тон напарника: любая ошибка/пропуск подаётся как нейтральный факт, а не оценка человека.



# 🎬 4 самых важных аспекта композиции в UX UI дизайне — UPROCK


### Пользователь сканирует в поисках якорей; дизайнер строит траекторию взгляда  ·  [03:46](https://youtu.be/RXlAyFbamh0?t=226)
**Мысль:** Задача дизайнера в том, чтобы человек получал максимум за минимум энергии. Он не читает, а ищет якоря, поэтому нужна понятная линия сверху вниз.

> Globally, the designer's task is to create a design that meets the user's need to gain the maximum while spending the minimum amount of energy. And how can we achieve that? By default, the user scans your design. What does that mean? They aren't reading; they are looking in a unfocused way, searching for anchors. And they still have a certain point around which they direct their focus. It focuses like this—when they see something interesting, they click and focus, and they can move it around like this, here, there, however they want. See? And our task is not to strain the person's imagination, but to make a clear, hyper-logical line of direction—the optimal trajectory for the gaze.

**Для Напарника:** Экран Фокуса строить как одну вертикальную линию взгляда с якорями: шаг, таймер, действие.



# 🎬 5 Game Design Secrets That Fix Retention & LTV — Kutovaya Elena


### Сегментируй по мотивации, а не по поведению; SDT как простая модель  ·  [01:42](https://youtu.be/EAfYEhCve6Q?t=102)
**Мысль:** Каждое действие в продукте либо закрывает потребность, либо нет; без потребности человек приходит только по необходимости. Простейшая модель SDT: автономия, компетентность, принадлежность.

> User psychology. Product analytics usually splits users by their behavior. How often they visit? How did they go in the funnel? How much they spend. Game design splits users differently. It start with motivation. These are two very different approaches and they lead to very different product architectures. Here is the basic tool, a model that sorts players into groups based on what motivates them. There are similar models for products too. They point to a few core types of motivation. Your job as a product architect is to figure out how your product meets each one. Every action in your product either meets one of these needs or it meets none of them. If a feature doesn't give the user what they are after, they'll come back only when they really have to. They won't get pulled in. They definitely won't be happy around it. That means lower engagement, low retention, lower altitude. Gamified systems follow one simple rule. Give every type of user a way to meet their needs. Motivation should shape everything. Your features, your tone of voice, your UI UIX, even how your design, your offers. If you don't keep every user type in view, some of them will just drop off. Not because they're the wrong users, because your product has no depth for them, no triggers built for them. And that shows up as retention decrease. and exactly those segments. The easiest model to learn and to use is SDT, selfdetermination theory. A huge number of modern products already run on it. It names just three core motivations. Autonomy, competence, and social belonging. Find out how your users meet each of them in your product.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять каждый экран Напарника по SDT: даёт ли он автономию (выбор), компетентность (прогресс острова), принадлежность (кот рядом).

> It down to my favorites or things that I really love because they're fun they're easy to use they're very error tolerant or forgiving so if you don't use it for a while you can easily come back to it without any judgment without any sort of penalty to having missed a day or a week or a year.

**Для Напарника:** Прямое требование к Напарнику: возвращение после паузы встречается без счётчика пропусков и без упрёка.


### Планирование для устойчивой продуктивности: делай меньше, чтобы сделать больше  ·  [06:12](https://youtu.be/kzxNGPJROcA?t=372)
**Мысль:** План помогает избежать выгорания, переносить то, что может подождать; чем больше на тарелке, тем реже всё выполняется.

> Planning your week is a tool that is very underestimated in the adhc community and I underestimate it all the time and I keep thinking but I don't have time to plan my week I have too much to do planning requires executive function which is impaired in ADHD brains and so a lot of the time we need a tool that can help us do that and one of my favorites is Sama because it's not about planning so that you can get as much done as humanly possible it's about helping you be sustainably productive it's about helping you prevent burnout and to ensure that the things that you're working on are the things you want to be working on it walks you through step by step and it prompts you to push back things that can wait it is the first thing that I have found that actively encourages you to do less work because if you want to do more do less.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкая подсказка при перегрузе: «может, один шаг на сегодня?» вместо накопления списка.


### Недооценка времени ведёт к самобичеванию; измерение факта это лечит  ·  [07:18](https://youtu.be/kzxNGPJROcA?t=438)
**Мысль:** Мы думаем, что у нас больше времени и задачи короче, потом корим себя; фактическое время при выполнении показывает реальность.

> One of the big challenges that we have is that we think that we have more time than we do or we think that things will take less time than they actually do and then get frustrated with ourselves and beat ourselves up for not having gotten everything done when that was not actually possible so task number one let's say it's going to take 10 minutes then you hit play on the task it can bring you into Focus mode and it will tell you how long the task actually took this is a strategy that's really helpful for understanding how long things actually take you not how long you think they should take.

**Мысль:** Короткая задержка перед отвлечением тренирует терпимость к фокусу; блокировка напоминает вернуться.

> One thing that can help is something called a distractability delay like building and pauses to where okay we're going to get distracted but like what if we just like wait a little bit before we participate in that distraction that can help us build up our tolerance for staying focused on something and a lot of time that's all it takes for me you open up the app it's like nope sorry it's blocked and you're like ah right okay back to what I'm doing.

**Для Напарника:** Если человек выходит из Фокуса, напарник мягко задерживает: «ещё минутка?» без блокировки и без стыда.


### Чеклист освобождает рабочую память: с часа до 15 минут на выход из дома  ·  [10:23](https://youtu.be/kzxNGPJROcA?t=623)
**Мысль:** Чеклист оставляет рабочую память на действие, а не на припоминание; делается за 5 минут и экономит часы.

> The beautiful thing about checklist is it makes it so you're you're able to use your working memory for the doing part as opposed to the remembering what to do part it lightens the mental load so much and makes life so much less overwhelming because you just have to follow the checklist so many times I'm leaving the house and I run back in because I forgot something and then I run out and then I'm like wait what am I forgetting I feel like I'm forgetting something no I think I have everything and then I get halfway to work and I'm like oh that's what I forgot I need a checklist it felt like this big ordeal to make a checklist because like sitting down and figuring out what I need to do and then writing it down it turns out making a checklist is really quick and if you forget something on your checklist you can add it to the checklist when you realize that you forgot it I went from taking an hour to get out the door in the morning to 15 minutes and oh my God one checklist one that took me 5 minutes to make has saved me probably hours this week.

**Для Напарника:** Шаблоны ритуалов («выйти из дома», «начать рабочий день») как готовые очереди шагов.



# 🎬 9 || Accessibility in UI/UX design for beginners || English — Dezinx by Kirtanya


### Ограничение бывает постоянным, временным и ситуативным  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/J38z28N7MoU?t=22)
**Мысль:** Три вида: постоянное (одна рука), временное (травма), ситуативное (в руках вещи). Проектировать надо под все три, а не только под диагноз.

> Now accessibility simply means designing for people who have some special need like they might be having some physical disability or mental disability or they might be in a special situation or they have some special requirements because disability is not permanent like we have three kind of disability permanent temporary and situational you can see the example of this thing you can take the screenshot of this thing like if you take the example of touch imagine if a person who have only one hand so he will use any app or website with only one hand or in any situation if you got injured then in that case also the user can use only one hand or you might be carrying something in your hand so in these three conditions the person is disabled either temporary permanently or situationally so you have to design your app according to these things.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник для людей с СДВГ: учитывать ситуативную невнимательность (шум, спешка, одна рука); крупные цели нажатия, минимум ввода текста.



# 🎬 A History and Future of AR and VR | Amber Case | AR in Action — AR in ACTION

**Для Напарника:** Мерить экраны Напарника не только кликами, но и когнитивной нагрузкой: число решений на шаг.



# 🎬 A Lesson in How Inconsistent UI Kills the First Experience | App Breakdown #88 — App Breakdown


### Лавина всплывающих подсказок: «просто дай мне увидеть приложение»  ·  [10:06](https://youtu.be/hgpk0ejGs-E?t=606)
**Мысль:** Много подсказок, всплывающих отовсюду, перегружают и вызывают желание уйти. Прогрессивное раскрытие без объяснения зачем — тоже шум.

> And I'm actually going to stop. Like, I've been shown so many pop-ups. I just want to be on the home screen and it's just stuff appearing everywhere. It's just from here, from there. Like, what is happening? Just let me see the app for myself.

**Для Напарника:** Никаких туров и всплывашек в Напарнике (и никаких toast): одна тёплая реплика за визит, остальное человек открывает сам.



# 🎬 ADHD & Time Blindness — Russell Barkley, PhD - Dedicated to ADHD Science+


### Баркли: время они воспринимают, но не могут использовать для действий  ·  [02:49](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=169)
**Мысль:** В экспериментах люди с СДВГ довольно точно оценивали длительность интервала, но проваливались при воспроизведении: восприятие времени не управляет поведением. Отсюда «слепота ко времени» как провал саморегуляции, а не сенсорики.

> But what we were doing is we were presenting time intervals to people with ADHD Seth was studying children we were also doing this again with teens and with adults and we would do this in three ways we would present we'd turn a light bulb on turn it off and say how long was that and that's sort of your perception of time the second thing that we would do is to have we would turn on a light bulb all right and then turn it off and we would say now you do that turn that light bulb on for that period of time and here's what we found their perception of time wasn't so bad they were pretty close to the typical people in the control group in telling us how long an interval was they're not perfect but they weren't quite as bad at it where they really fell apart was in duplicating the time interval well what does that mean it means that even if I can perceive time I can't use it to guide my motor action my behavior now a task is called time reproduction can you reproduce a time interval and they couldn't and we found that this was typical of young children older children teens adults it was bad across the lifespan that we were testing.

**Для Напарника:** Не полагаться на «чувство времени» пользователя: показывать время наружу (визуальный таймер сессии, остров/кот как внешний отсчёт), а не просить «следить за часами».


### Ожидание как пытка: delay aversion при слепоте ко времени  ·  [07:20](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=440)
**Мысль:** Если время ощущается как текущее медленно или стоящее, ожидание вызывает нетерпение, фрустрацию и импульсивные действия, чтобы прервать задержку. Поведение — следствие, а не «плохой характер».

> When it comes to situations in which people with ADHD have to wait for things now there's a waiting interval here and because if you are blind to time you perceive time as moving slowly or not at all then what's going to happen if you are asked to wait for something you're going to become incredibly impatient in that situation if you're driving and you're asked to wait in line because maybe there's some construction going on these are the kind of people who are more likely to drive on the shoulder of the road or to get up on the sidewalk and to try to make that right hand turn and get around the obstacle because it's painful for them to wait for that period of time I mean it's as if they can't perceive the time and when they what they sense is that it's going very slowly and they're just not going to wait around for it I mean as one of my patients said you know asking someone with ADHD to wait is their definition of Hell well it certainly is so expect that for children for adults who have to wait in lines for things or just wait around for something to begin expect to see them become more Restless more impatient more frustrated perhaps been more angry and aggressive in those situations in order to terminate that delay my friend Edward sonugubark in England has referred to this particular problem as being delay averse a very good term for what's going on here.

**Для Напарника:** Экраны загрузки/ожидания (дробление задачи через ИИ, старт сессии) должны быть короткими и живыми: кот что-то делает, а не пустой спиннер; никакого «подождите» без присутствия.


### Событие-реакция-исход: пока разрыв короткий, лобная кора не нужна  ·  [09:19](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=559)
**Мысль:** Если событие, реакция и последствие следуют вплотную (как в видеоигре), СДВГ не мешает; как только между ними вставлен временной интервал, нужна функция «связывания времени» лобных долей, которую СДВГ-мозг тянет хуже. Вот почему игры держат часами, а отложенные задачи — нет.

> The way we look at situations and tasks is going to be important we need to think about tasks and situations as coming to us as what I call ero Arrangements events things coming toward us responses how we prepare for the things that are coming at us in time and consequences the outcomes how do we do were we ready on time or not so ero Arrangements now here's the thing about ADHD right if these ero arrangements are together very closely there's hardly any time between them it's kind of like a video game right then guess what no problem you don't need a frontal lobe to deal with event response outcome Arrangements many organisms can do this particularly animals can do this but other organisms can as well they are schenerian creatures like BF Skinner they respond to stimulus response stimulus response right so if these arrangements are tight together in time your frontal lobe isn't necessary and we can do them easily this is why people with ADHD can play video games for hours and do so effectively because the video game has arranged this kind of very addictive schedule of consequences for engaging with the task and with the environment but the minute you insert time between any of these three parts the minute you insert an interval between the event and the response required for the event or between my response and finding out how I did the outcome you need a frontal lobe because as Joaquin Foster said after studying the frontal lobes of primates all of his career if we can reduce the functions of the frontal lobe in the executive brain to one essential idea it is The Binding of events over time in mind so as to deal with the future we are a Time binding animal nobody no other species does it the way we do so if you put time in these Arrangements you are going to disable the person with ADHD.

**Для Напарника:** Главный принцип для Напарника: сократить задержку между действием и наградой. Каждый шаг сессии должен получать немедленный видимый отклик (рост острова, реакция кота), а не отложенный итог в конце дня.


### Книжный отчёт: как сжать разрывы и добавить немедленные последствия  ·  [11:49](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=709)
**Мысль:** Долгий проект с дедлайнами через недели «обезоруживает» подростка с СДВГ. Решение: ежедневная маленькая порция (пара страниц, пересказ, запись) и сразу положительное последствие после каждого короткого рабочего отрезка.

> A good example of that is a book report for a child or a teen with ADHD the teacher says okay I want you to read Catcher in the Rye you've got several weeks to do this and in two weeks I want to see an outline a week after that I want to see the book report and a week after that you'll get your grade after I've graded all the class papers wow boy did you just disable that teen with ADHD it's not going to happen his parents are going to have to do this or his parents are going to have to ride hurt on him all the time during that time to make sure that this gets done in time over time on time but they're not going to be able to do this on their own the way another teen might be able to organize themselves over time to get that book report done so I hope you understand that the more we put ourselves in situations and tasks that have time inserted between these components the more people with ADHD will struggle to succeed in that situation the more likely they are to fail so what's the answer minimize these time gaps try to bring the E and the r and EO back together as close as you can we can't make life a video game but we can certainly break the task down get rid of some of these time delays and therefore make the person with ADHD more effective in that situation let's go back to that book report what could you do you could every day have that child or teen read one or two pages recite out loud what was important write it down on a sheet of paper read that back out loud again that's called the oh I can't think of the name of it right now sorry about that but it involves this kind of oh it's sq4r I'm sorry it's my 73 year old brain getting to me here sq4r so you survey read recite write review okay so we're going to do that with this book and we're to do this every day and then the work starts to get done but notice what we did we shortened the project made it into small quotas with very little breaks more review and the consequences are immediate if you will read the two pages if you will take notes of what's important you will get X tokens points maybe additional allowance or money but arrange a positive event after each of these little short work periods so bring the events and responses and outcomes together.

**Для Напарника:** Дробление задачи (api/breakdown) должно давать микро-квоты на сегодня, а не недельный план; после каждого микрошага — мгновенная награда на острове, без накопления до финала.


### Внешние таймеры вместо внутренних часов; напоминания о сроке не помогают  ·  [14:38](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=878)
**Мысль:** Внутренние часы не ведут поведение, поэтому время нужно вынести наружу (таймеры, вибрация, календари). При этом простые напоминания «ты сделал?» бесполезны — работает только разбиение на маленькие ежедневные единицы.

> Smaller quotas but that you have an external time device the internal clock in the brain isn't working it's not guiding them even if it is working external timing devices are a very helpful here's one over at Amazon it's a one foot clock that goes up to an hour and you can set the dial for whatever length of time you want and then the red disc is disappearing as time is passing isn't that wonderful you can use that for ADHD kids and teens even adults on their desk in their workspace to give them an external reference for the passage of time and that will help them with guiding their behavior relative to that time interval but you don't have to use that Amazon clock you can use cooking timers you can use your cell phone you can use technology like this laptop computer you can use watches that may have a vibrating function in them and set them for certain time intervals you can use the watchminder in which you can put things you have to get done over time and it will remind you on time any way you can from you know your outlook Calendar or your Google calendar to whatever that you use to manage time people with ADH are going to be more Reliant and dependent on external timing devices we're going to offload time onto other devices for larger tasks break them into small quotas as I said and do that quota very frequently so a little bit each day over time builds the bridge across the chasm of time and gets us to the other side but don't just point at that time interval and say did you do your summer reading have you done your book report reminders about time are not helpful for this condition but breaking the task down into small units and little Eros is going to be very helpful.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает визуальный таймер в Фокусе. Предупреждение для уведомлений: не слать «ты не сделал X», а предлагать крошечный следующий шаг (не применимо: напоминания-укоры, это противоречит тону без стыда).


### Социальная подотчётность повышает вероятность довести дело до конца  ·  [16:45](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=1005)
**Мысль:** Рядом с людьми, лучше управляющими временем, и перед кем-то подотчётным (коллега, куратор), шанс выполнить намерение выше, чем при «сделаю про себя».

> You can also Place yourself around other people who manage time better and work with them in small groups in teams or make yourself accountable to another student a colleague a supervisor as to what you want to get done within that time and then check in with them and show them that you've got it done there's nothing like social accountability for teens and adults to make you more likely to follow through on something than if you just said I'm going to do this now and nobody knows that you're not accountable for that.

**Для Напарника:** Кот-напарник как присутствие: можно «сообщить» ему намерение перед сессией и вернуться отчитаться; без надзора и стыда, только тёплое «ты сделал» или «ничего страшного».



# 🎬 ADHD and Focus? Here's What Works — Kati Morton


### Point of performance: подсказка ровно там, где срываешься  ·  [00:25](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=25)
**Мысль:** Напоминания и инструменты нужно ставить в точку, где человек отвлекается или перегружается (чеклист на двери гаража).

> This is where the concept of point of performance becomes invaluable point of performance means setting up reminders or tools in the exact spot where you're likely to get distracted or overwhelmed an example of this would be like posting a checklist on the garage door of things that you need to bring to work each day that ensures that you remember everything before leaving by using this technique you're giving yourself tools that help you stay focused exactly when distractions are most likely to occur.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка Напарника в момент застревания внутри Фокуса, а не заранее в настройках.


### Раздели и победи: пример тренировки из пяти микрошагов, каждый даёт чувство выполненного  ·  [04:52](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=292)
**Мысль:** Каждый завершённый мелкий пункт даёт ощущение достижения и подпитывает мотивацию; отвлекающий пункт (плейлист) лучше решить заранее.

> Strategy number three divide and conquer for those of us with ADHD breaking tasks down into smaller bite-sized steps is one of the most effective ways to stay motivated and productive instead of looking at a giant task that feels overwhelming divide and conquer it by turning it into a series of small achievable steps for example if you want to work out your list could look like number one I've got to find my headphones number two I got to put my workout clothes on number three I'll pick my playlist now this one could be tricky and we could get distracted so maybe pick that out ahead of time but number four put your shoes on number five start your workout as you finish each one of those items you'll feel a sense of accomplishment which helps keep you motivated.

**Для Напарника:** Пример-шаблон для breakdown (найти наушники, надеть форму, выбрать плейлист заранее) и маленькая награда за каждый шаг.


### Будильник нельзя выключить, пока не начал: правило с собой  ·  [05:48](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=348)
**Мысль:** Назойливая немедленность будильника создаёт срочность для старта.

> If you find you have trouble getting started on one task or a piece of a task you can also set alarms for things that you want to accomplish pick a time and set the alarm and then make a rule with yourself you cannot turn off that alarm until you've actually started the task the immediacy of that alarm also kind of the Annoying piece of it works with our ADHD brains giving us that little push of urgency that we sometimes need to get started.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо в виде назойливого сигнала; можно мягкий вариант: напарник тихо зовёт, пока шаг не начат.


### Почему body doubling работает: присутствие как якорь и тихий болельщик  ·  [07:32](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=452)
**Мысль:** Присутствие другого создаёт подотчётность, внешнюю структуру, зеркалирование и ощущение команды, даже без общей задачи.

> Why does this work so well for ADHD brains for starters ADHD often comes with issues around Focus accountability and motivation it can feel like we have a million tabs open in our heads and it's easy to get distracted overwhelmed or lost in procrastination when someone else is there even if they're not working on the same task just their presence can help anchor us to the task at hand it creates like a sense of accountability even if no one is directly monitoring what we're doing it's like having a silent cheerleader next to us reminding us to stay on task body doubling helps create structure many of us with ADHD Thrive when there's external structure and cues to help us Focus knowing that someone else is there creates a natural boundary reducing the temptation to drift off into distractions there's also this bonus of social pressure like in a positive way if someone else is working it can Inspire us to keep going too almost like we're mirroring their productivity body doubling Taps into the idea that we're more motivated when we feel like we're part of a team even if we're doing separate tasks this technique helps break through the isolation and difficulty in self-starting that can often come along with ADHD in short body doubling turns a solo task into something shared making it easier and often more fun to get things done.

**Для Напарника:** Главное обоснование кота у костра: молчаливый болельщик рядом; можно усилить мелкими реакциями, когда человек начал шаг.


### Мостовые утверждения: от «я тупая» к «возможно, не самая тупая»  ·  [08:59](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=539)
**Мысль:** Заметить самокритику, проверить факты и постепенно смягчать формулировки шаг за шагом - реалистичнее резкой смены на позитив.

> Strategy number six practice self-compassion this is one of the most important things you can do if you have ADHD and here's why many of us with ADHD struggle not just with Focus but with low self-esteem and deep feelings of Shame we've likely spent years hearing things like why are you so lazy or you're just not trying hard enough over time this can lead to a harsh inner voice that criticizes us every time we make a mistake or fall behind this self talk can be incredibly damaging to practice self-compassion I want you to do these three things notice when the negative selft talk is happening check your facts and use Bridge statements noticing when it's happening means that we pay more attention to the thoughts we're having about ourselves at this step you don't really have to take any action you're just turning your attention to the thoughts that you have regularly checking the facts means that we don't accept our thoughts at face value an example of this would be that instead of thinking I'm so lazy and accepting that as true I would look to see if I had any evidence to support it and remember thoughts are not facts utilizing Bridge statements means that we're slowly challenging our thoughts and moving them into a more positive place an example of using a bridge statement would be that instead of thinking GH I'm so stupid I would say it's possible that I might not be the dumbest person in the world see not as positive but none near nearly as negative and trust me when I tell you that you will feel that difference each Bridge statement we make should be a little less negative than the one before it's like we're building a bridge plank by plank towards a more positive place.

**Для Напарника:** Реплики напарника, когда человек ругает себя: не «ты молодец!», а мягкий мостик («возможно, сегодня просто тяжело»).


### Самосострадание не снимает ответственность, а создаёт пространство для роста  ·  [10:41](https://youtu.be/Mx6YlI19bL0?t=641)
**Мысль:** Без груза вины быстрее возвращаешься в колею; доброта к себе повышает устойчивость.

> Self-compassion isn't about letting yourself off the hook it's about being kind to yourself in the process when you stop shaming yourself for every small mistake you create the space to grow and improve without the weight of guilt this in turn boosts your resilience and helps you get back on track faster it's like learning to be your own biggest supporter rather than your harshest critic.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает принцип «без вины»: возврат после срыва должен быть тёплым и быстрым.



# 🎬 ADHD and Losing Track of Time: Why Your Day Disappears + What Helps — GenX ADHD


### Дрейф: мозг недостимулирован и ищет следующую задачу  ·  [01:08](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=68)
**Мысль:** Неструктурированное время превращается в дрейф — человек не закончил задачу, заскучал и ищет новое интересное; дело не в хаосе, а в недостимуляции.

> I didn't even know what drift was until the last few years, and now that I understand what it is, I realize that most of my unstructured time I was actually in drift. And I would joke around and I would say, you know, oh, it's just ADHD chaos. Like, I start off the day and eventually I get stuff done, but I just kind of go with the flow and move from task to task to task to task. Now, I know what that is called. It is called drift, and essentially my brain was under stimulated, and I was looking for the next engaging task. So, even though I wasn't finished this task, I was bored with it, and I went looking for the next task.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать в чате, что дрейф — нормально, и мягко возвращать к одному шагу, а не ругать.


### Capture space: одно постоянное место для идей и задач  ·  [01:53](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=113)
**Мысль:** Структура против дрейфа — место, куда всегда записывается важное; оно не должно быть изысканным, главное — доступное и всегда одно.

> So, how do we handle drift? By having a plan. We need to give ourselves structure. Now, not necessarily like work level structure, but we do need to give ourselves structure. So, this is where the capture space is introduced. So, this is a designated place where you can write down your ideas, your tasks, your to-do's, anything like that's important. You should ideally have two, one for home or one for work. And it does not need to be anything fancy. You don't need to go buy a new planner or anything like that. It needs to be accessible and you always need to the same place.

**Для Напарника:** Быстрый «поймать мысль» в Напарнике: отдельное поле в Доме, чтобы идея не уводила из сессии.


### План на день: три приоритета, не пять-восемь  ·  [02:37](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=157)
**Мысль:** Заранее выбрать три дела и сам решить, с какого начать; три — достаточно структуры, не перегрузка.

> The daily plan. So, this is how we stop drift. So, before the day starts, make a plan. And it does not need to be an elaborate plan. My work week is actually very highly structured because I am a therapist and I have a very consistent schedule. I know what my day's like before it starts because I see pretty much the same client. So, every Wednesday I see this client at 9:00, I see this client at 10:00. It's always the same. Where I drift is on the weekends. So, if I'm setting up a Saturday schedule, I'm thinking about my Saturday schedule on Thursday or Friday. So, I might set up three priorities. I'm not going to do five, six, seven, or eight. Three priorities. So, you know, it could be getting laundry done or maybe it is getting the kitchen clean. So, like I do a kitchen reset every Saturday morning. It's part of my daily priorities for Saturday. sometimes I might have some extra ones. So, maybe in the afternoon I need to take the cat to the vet or I'm going to the grocery store, but that is part of my priority list. So, I'm going to pick three things that I know I need to get done on Saturday and I'm going to write those out and those become my structure and my plan for Saturday.

**Для Напарника:** План на день на Доме ограничить тремя пунктами; ноль пунктов — тоже нормальный день, без стыда.


### Нужны и рамки времени, и рамки задачи  ·  [04:25](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=265)
**Мысль:** Автор с СДВГ: открытое завершение вредно; одна задача (кухня) и одно время (30 минут) удерживают внимание.

> Time and task containment. So, this is a different approach than what you're going to see a lot of other ADHD coaches or therapists or different productivity people suggest. over my years of working with people with ADHD, but also having ADHD, we need time containment, but we also need task containment. We cannot have everything being open-ended. We need structure. So, when I am doing my kitchen reset, I am only doing my kitchen reset. I'm not cleaning the living room, I'm not cleaning the bathroom, I'm not doing anything else, I'm only doing the kitchen. And I know from my personal experience it takes me about 30 minutes to reset the kitchen, I'm going to get that done. So, my task, the kitchen, time, about 30 minutes. That is contained.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса: одна задача + одна длительность, всё остальное скрыто.


### Таймер учит оценивать, сколько реально занимает задача  ·  [05:22](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=322)
**Мысль:** Таймер 30 минут и 5-минутный перерыв с вопросом «успел ли?» тренируют чувство времени и оценку задач.

> And if you are just learning to be able to do task and time containment, then you're going to want to set a timer cuz you're going to want to start figuring out how long it actually takes you to do some of these tasks. So, I recommend setting a timer for 30 minutes, picking your task, working on it. When the timer goes off, step back, take a 5-minute break, kind of re-evaluate, like did get the task finished before the timer went off? Do I still need another 5 minutes? This is you starting to learn time, but it's also you starting to learn how long it takes you to do task.

**Для Напарника:** После сессии кот спрашивает «хватило?» и запоминает реальную длительность, чтобы предлагать точнее.


### Смена задач с переходом против скуки  ·  [05:55](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=355)
**Мысль:** Чередование предметов блоками по 30 минут с 5–10-минутными перерывами работает лучше двух часов одного, но только если есть переход (пройтись, попить).

> If this is a work-related task, one of the things that I recommend is actually task switching. So, let's say you work on a task for an hour. So, you say, "I'm going to work on payroll for 1 hour, and that's all I'm going to work on." When that timer goes off, you give yourself a break, you get up, you stretch, you go use the restroom, you drink some water, and then you come back, and unless the payroll is urgent, you should switch task. This is going to help prevent your brain from getting bored. And that's one of the biggest things with ADHD, and the reason why we struggle is because we get bored, and our brain doesn't want to do it anymore. when I am working with teenagers in therapy, and they are struggling with getting homework done, we will set up a schedule where they do English for 30 minutes, 5 to 10-minute break, math for 30 minutes, 5 to 10-minute break. And they keep doing that alternating until they either complete the assignments, or maybe if it's a project, they got some work done, but it isn't finished, and that's okay because they were able to get done. And most of the teenagers that I work with who use that strategy report being able to get more work done because they don't feel doomed to just sit and only do math for 2 hours, or only do English for 2 hours. Like, the task switching actually helps break it up, but it has to be task switching with a transition. So, math, take a break, walk around the house,.

**Для Напарника:** После сессии предлагать перерыв-переход и, при желании, другую задачу; кот предлагает встать и пройтись.


### Staging: не носить предмет через комнаты, чтобы не дрейфовать  ·  [07:53](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=473)
**Мысль:** Оставить вещь по пути, а не относить в другую комнату, чтобы не сменить контекст и не уплыть.

> And the other thing that I do is I stage items. So, maybe if there is an item in the kitchen that doesn't belong there, I'm not going to walk it all the way to the room that it goes, I'm going to stage it in the direction that it belongs. And this is in order to keep my brain in that task. Cuz if I walk into another room, I'm going to start to drift and that's the goal. I'm trying not to drift.

**Для Напарника:** Идея шагов: не предлагать сопутствующие переходы в процессе шага.


### Правило возврата: записать идею и «где я остановился»  ·  [08:15](https://youtu.be/a0FjynFfQGo?t=495)
**Мысль:** Идея записывается за 10 секунд и не уводит в кроличью нору; запись «на чём остановился» убирает страх не найти путь обратно к задаче.

> The return rule. So, if you are working on something and an idea pops into your head, write it in your capture space. Takes 10 seconds and it's better than going down a whole new rabbit hole. I already mentioned about the staging items and then let's say you are working on a task and the timer goes off and it's time to take a break, you can quickly write a return note in your capture space so you know where you are. Part of the problem with us that we struggle leaving a task is because we're scared we won't remember how to get back into it. Like we don't have a return. So, if you write down in your capture space the last thing I was working on as I was typing this note for this client, then I'm going to know where I was.

**Для Напарника:** При паузе/выходе из сессии сохранять и показывать «Ты остановился здесь: …» на возврате (Zeigarnik).



# 🎬 ADHD and Motivation — How to ADHD


### Мост мотивации: у СДВГ половина «досок» отсутствует, это не лень  ·  [00:11](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=11)
**Мысль:** Разрыв между намерением и действием у большинства закрывается мотивацией; при СДВГ важность задачи не даёт достаточно «досок», поэтому сила воли не работает, а окружающие ошибочно видят лень.

> We don’t always do what we intend to do. Why? Because between intending to do it and actually doing it, there's a big chasm. With most tasks, for most people, that chasm is bridged by motivation. We’ll call this “Motivation Bridge.” And it’s built using motivational planks like “This will be good for my career.” “It will make my family proud.” “I told a bunch of people I would.” Motivation bridge isn’t always perfect, but little gaps in motivation can be overcome with will power. Self-discipline. I don’t want to do the thing, but if it’s important to me, I will. I might not have all the motivation, but I have enough that I can get there. Unfortunately, ADHD is what Dr. Russell Barkley often refers to a “motivation deficit disorder.” Something might be just as important to us as it is to someone without ADHD but that importance doesn’t give us as many motivational planks. We don’t have little gaps in motivation. Half the bridge is missing. Which means we might want to cross that chasm and do the thing we might put a ton of effort into trying to. We just…can’t. This can be really confusing and frustrating for people who can’t see all the missing planks and don’t understand why we don’t just cross the bridge and do the thing. They can get over their gaps, why can’t we get over ours?

**Для Напарника:** Ключевая рамка для текстов без стыда: невозможность начать подаётся как нехватка опор, а не как вина человека.


### Что мотивирует СДВГ: срочное, новое, интересное  ·  [01:57](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=117)
**Мысль:** Мозг СДВГ плохо справляется с длинным, повторяющимся и скучным; зажигают срочность, новизна и личный интерес; игры дают это по умолчанию.

> According to research, ADHD brains tend to have trouble with tasks that are lengthy, repetitive, or boring. On the flip side, there are a few things that give ADHD brains a bunch of motivational planks: Things that are urgent. Things that are new, or novel And things that are of personal interest. In other words, things that are stimulating, or engaging to our brains. Which is also why stimulant medication and exercise can be helpful. This need for stimulation isn’t a choice, it’s how our brains work. Some tasks naturally include these motivational planks -- Video games, anyone? Which is why they hold our focus so well. But for those that don’t, we can add them.

**Для Напарника:** Для каждой механики Напарника проверять, даёт ли она срочность, новизну или интерес; недостающее добавлять.


### Искусственные дедлайны слабы, помогает безстыдная подотчётность  ·  [02:53](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=173)
**Мысль:** Выдуманный дедлайн не ощущается реальным, а подотчётность другому человеку делает его настоящим; «сдай когда сможешь» отнимает опоры, лучше мягко спрашивать о прогрессе.

> Creating artificial deadlines? Is kinda hit-and-miss for ADHD brains, since we know on some level the deadline isn’t “real” -- but accountability can help make it real. Maybe instead of having one final deadline for a paper, ask your teacher if you can have a deadline for each draft. If you’ve got a big project at work, meet with a coworker regularly to check in on each other’s progress. Wanna get your house clean? Invite someone over. Knowing someone will see our house -- and soon can give us the motivational planks we need to clean it up. And sometimes we don’t even need to make the task urgent, we just need to see the ways in which it already is. I might not feel like I need to do something today, but if I don’t, it’s gonna put me behind on the rest of my week. Realizing that helps. Timers are also useful. Setting a timer and seeing how much you can get done before it goes off. What doesn’t help? Seeing someone with ADHD struggling with something and being like “it’s okay, get it to me whenever.” Although it’s wonderfully understanding, it actually takes away planks and makes it less likely that we’ll be able to get it done. Instead, check in. Ask for updates. Shame-free accountability is really helpful.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник может мягко спрашивать «как идёт?» вместо отложенных дедлайнов; тон проверки без осуждения.


### Новизна: новая обстановка, новые способы, итерация, геймификация  ·  [03:50](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=230)
**Мысль:** Если задачу нельзя сделать новой, можно менять среду и способ выполнения; повторяющиеся дела оживляет геймификация.

> 2. Make it new, or novel We can’t always do new things, but we can do them in new environments or in new ways. Do your homework in a hammock. Learn new ways to fold laundry. I was kinda meh about folding laundry until I learned Marie Kondo’s method, and now it’s kinda fun. If you’re having trouble getting yourself to floss, try getting a variety of floss flavors to choose from. I tend to get bored of my hobbies really quickly, but I’ve learned to accept that once they get old, I will put them down for a while and I’ll cycle back to them when they feel new again. For tasks you have to do a lot, you can iterate on them -- challenge yourself to find easier, better or faster ways to do them. Or weirder ways! Studying might be boring, but what if I had to do it upside down? Gamification can make repetitive tasks novel.

**Для Напарника:** Вариативность в реакциях напарника и редкие находки на острове, чтобы повторяющиеся сессии оставались свежими.


### Награды должны быть немедленными, дальние не работают  ·  [05:22](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=322)
**Мысль:** У людей с СДВГ иное восприятие времени, поэтому долгосрочные награды слабы; мгновенная обратная связь сокращает расстояние между действием и наградой.

> Whatever you do, try not to make a task you need to get done compete with one that’s more interesting. Even if we can’t find anything else interesting about a task, we can add rewards that are interesting. Rewards can be especially motivating for ADHD brains, but keep in mind, we experience time differently. Long-term rewards are often too far away to be effective. This is why the immediate feedback of sticker charts, raffle tickets, or even a quick verbal acknowledgement is great. It shortens the distance between the behavior and the reward, which makes it more motivating. And gives us more motivational planks.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает мгновенную награду после каждой сессии (хаптика, реакция кота, рост острова), а не недельные цели.


### Не убеждай в важности - выясни, чего не хватает, и добавь опор  ·  [06:05](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=365)
**Мысль:** Напоминания о важности бесполезны: человек и так знает; нужно любопытство к тому, чего не хватает.

> But if your bridge looks like this… Don’t waste energy trying to jump that gap or telling yourself or someone you love how important it is that they get it done. They know. Instead, get curious about what’s missing -- And then? Fill in the planks.

**Для Напарника:** Не давить морализаторскими фразами в чате напарника; вместо этого спрашивать, что мешает начать, и предлагать меньший шаг.



# 🎬 ADHD and Time-Blocking: How does it work?! — Evie Ran


### Записанная задача менее абстрактна, а неоднозначность особенно мучительна при СДВГ  ·  [00:44](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=44)
**Мысль:** Видимый список снижает неопределённость и превращает задачи в конкретные вещи, которые легче выполнить.

> Well for two main reasons first is that you get to visualize your tasks when you have your things written down in front of you they are less abstract they're less ambiguous and just about anybody struggles with ambiguity but for ADHD people this is particularly frustrating so if you can reduce the ambiguity and increase the objective factual real things in front of you such as your tasks then you increase your chances to execute them.

**Для Напарника:** Шаг в Фокусе формулировать конкретно и глагольно, без двусмысленности («открыть файл», а не «заняться отчётом»).


### Классический тайм-блокинг не работает: дофамин меняется ото дня ко дню  ·  [02:20](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=140)
**Мысль:** Нельзя заранее знать, какие задачи получатся в какое время; жёсткая сетка либо наскучит, либо замучит.

> Why classical time blocking doesn't work is because as an ad person you have different levels of dopamine per day and it's very difficult for you to decide how you're going to feel tomorrow at what time of the day you'll be able to execute what kind of tasks because classical time blocking looks something like this you have your calendar and you have everyday scheduled part tasks and within a certain time frame and the classical time blocking that you can learn a lot about from somebody like Ali abdal for example can really help you with productivity absolutely but for ADHD people it's extremely difficult to stay true to such rigid structure in the long term because as somebody who drives on challenge interest and deadlines having such rigid time blocking schedule will either bore you to b or make you very miserable.

**Для Напарника:** Не строить жёсткий календарь в Напарнике; выбор шага и длительности должен быть гибким в моменте.


### Не полагайся на силу воли - убери искушение  ·  [04:38](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=278)
**Мысль:** Успех проще делать лёгким, убирая соблазны, чем пытаться сильнее держаться.

> I don't believe in willpower I don't think you can achieve that much just because you're very self-disciplined I think you can achieve a lot more when you make the success easier than when you keep it complicated and try harder to overcome yourself like it's not worth the struggle keep the Temptation away from you and then just focus on what is in front of you.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка перед сессией «положи телефон подальше» и экран Фокуса без лишних ссылок и соблазнов.


### Планируй день утром 10-15 минут, а не надейся на настроение  ·  [05:25](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=325)
**Мысль:** Дневной план точнее долгосрочного и даёт осознанный старт вместо автопилота и надежды на настроение.

> For the daily task sit down in the morning and think about what would you like to do today what would you like to achieve today what would you like this day to look like and then make the list because first of all this is a lot more accurate than trying to plan it in advance but second of all this is how you start the day in a lot more intentional way because you're spending those 10 15 minutes literally designing your day versus running on autopilot hoping that the right mood will hit you at the right time and you achieve your stuff like honestly I've never seen a sustainable productive technique that relies on hope so okay you may be one of those extreme cases that can achieve that but if you're one of the 99% of the population on this Earth don't rely on Hope just sit down think about yourself think about your goals think about your responsibilities write it down and then go ahead and kick ass.

**Для Напарника:** Утренний ритуал на Доме: напарник предлагает один-два шага на сегодня, без обязательного плана (ноль - тоже нормально).


### Техники для СДВГ работают коротко: бери по нужде, не ждай идеальной продуктивности 24/7  ·  [07:16](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=436)
**Мысль:** Любая структура держится, пока нужна цель; ожидание вечной продуктивности ведёт к вине.

> This is important to also tell you and encourage you to recognize time management techniques very often for ADHD people are only possible in the short term I know You' like to hear that you can start some thing and execute it forever but when it comes to structuring your studying structuring your days and having time blocking even when you modify it usually it will last until you achieve your goals but it's very difficult to have that kind of living every single day just because it's good ad people have that difficult relationship with dopamine and with attention with executive functions it's unless you are medicated all the time to like regulate it then I would just advise you use techniques when need it and then keep parts of them for the rest of your life okay but do not expect from yourself to be that perfect productive person 24/7.

**Для Напарника:** Не выстраивать стрики и давление «каждый день»; перерывы допустимы, остров не наказывает.


### Цветовая градация задач: обязательства, дела, «было бы славно», радость  ·  [08:21](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=501)
**Мысль:** Разделение по связи с другим человеком и жёсткости времени снимает вопрос что можно двигать.

> Let's go into my color grading so I use four categories red was for responsibilities yellow for chores I'm going to explain the difference green was for it would be nice if I could and then purple was for things that make me happy now first of all what is the difference between category red responsibilities and category yellow chores responsibilities in red are things that are somehow connected to another human being your appointments at the dentist your work meeting even your appointments and dates with your spouse those are things that you have committed to and they are involving another person you must do them otherwise you're going to lose money respect relationships you're going to lose something because this other person is somehow involved this is red yellow on the other side is chores that you better do but nobody will come and knock on your door if you don't do it for example going to the groceries that's a chore cleaning your house that's a chore paying your rent is also a chore as long as you don't overdo your Rend day but whether you're going to do it on Monday or Tuesday usually doesn't matter whether you're going to do it in the morning or in the evening doesn't matter chores that are not exactly specified in terms of time in another person yellow.

**Для Напарника:** Тип шага (обязательство с другим человеком или свободный) можно различать мягко в очереди шагов и подбирать порядок.


### Минимум одно дело на радость в день  ·  [10:12](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=612)
**Мысль:** Мозг СДВГ не следует технике, если ему не интересно; в план нужно ставить хотя бы одну приятную вещь.

> And category number four was purple things that make me happy and here we have to explain a few things first it doesn't need to be purple actually in general I'm using Color grading because I'm very visual person but if you instead prefer other types of categorization let's say you're more geeky you can use the Harry Potter houses for example anything that will keep you entertained because your ADHD brain will fail to follow if it's not entertained so you take the technique and you make it fun for you if you are to stick with it and the second important thing about this category is that you should choose at least one purple item per day and that thing should be not hurtful to you.

**Для Напарника:** Дом предлагает не только шаг по задаче, но и мини-радость (чай у костра) как легитимный пункт дня.


### Радость не обязана быть полезной: избегай вредного, не требуй здорового  ·  [11:10](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=670)
**Мысль:** Цель - чувствовать себя хорошо, а не быть роботом; вред определяется масштабом, а не жанром.

> Pay attention being not hurtful is not the same as being healthy very often I hear those like used interchangeably and I'm just so f up with it we're not robots that's not our goal our goal is just to feel well and to achieve things that matter to us so something that is hurtful to you for example is to drink all day yeah alcohol hurts you okay but scrolling on Instagram for example for an hour does it hurt you it's arguable if it's during the day and it doesn't affect your sleep if it's like between tasks and you're having your aone time for an hour even having your coffee and just scrolling and depending also on your content like for example in my case most of my content that I'm fed up by the algum on Instagram is actually educational I love to watch things about physics and psychology or space and I learn from those things also I have bunch of cat videos they make me laugh but if you find yourself indulging into this for hours and hours then of course that will be hurting you so use your common sense to make the difference between hurting and being healthy it's okay if the things that make you happy are not necessarily healthy that's fine you allowed just avoid the hurting things because again the goal is to feel well.

**Для Напарника:** Тон без морализаторства: напарник не осуждает отдых и увлечения.


### Тяжёлое утром: утро/день/вечер как фильтр, а не расписание  ·  [13:20](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=800)
**Мысль:** Когнитивно тяжёлое, оставленное на поздний час, чаще не делается; грубые части дня задают направление, не сковывая.

> Also try to be a bit intentional with what time of the day you're going to do certain things because if you leave cognitively heavy tasks for later in the day there is a higher chance that you're not going to do it so I think about this a bit like in Netherlands and Belgium for example when you're trying to book a train ticket the app usually gives you options for like morning noon or evening trains and you kind of like set up this filter in advance so that you see the trains in certain category of the day and I kind of do the same with my tasks I'm like okay this is like morning afternoon evening and of course it's flexible.

**Для Напарника:** Подсказка напарника: «самое тяжёлое лучше пока свежо»; вечером предлагать лёгкие шаги.


### Слова меняют нагрузку: «пункт» вместо «задача»; автономия и гибкость делают систему реалистичной  ·  [14:17](https://youtu.be/-bUAku0FySI?t=857)
**Мысль:** Слово «задача» звучит корпоративно и давит; ощущение, что блоки можно двигать, поддерживает использование.

> I actually like to call them items because psychologically if I call it items it puts less stress on it versus task because task to me sounds very corporative and I'm like I don't like it but okay I have the visual items in front of me and to me they're like blocks and they're different colors so I kind of like could move them around you know and this is the flexibility I need I may not do it because I would try to stick with like the categories of morning afternoon and evening but the fact that I could and if something pops up I could just take this and move it here that thought that autonomy that flexibility makes it more realistic to me to keep up with this technique.

**Для Напарника:** Микрокопирайтинг: избегать слова «задача» в давящих контекстах; позволять переставлять шаги.



# 🎬 ADHD Body Doubling: Why You Can Only Focus With Someone There — Nicola Knobel (Audhd)


### Определение body doubling через бытовой пример с уборкой перед гостями  ·  [01:11](https://youtu.be/XLrVdLl5pP4?t=71)
**Мысль:** Узнаваемая ситуация: уборка вспыхивает, когда кто-то должен прийти, а без этого откладывается на дни. Body doubling — это выполнение задачи в присутствии другого человека, что делает возможными старт и завершение.

> Why can you clean the entire kitchen the second someone is coming to your house or a friend is showing up or you have a cleaning person coming around. Why do you clean the house before the cleaning person? Why? Why are you doing that? But you won't touch it for 3 days on your own. Why? I'm curious to know. Answer below. Anyway, the answer has a name and it is called body doubling and it means doing a task in the presence of another person so that starting it and finishing it suddenly becomes possible.

**Для Напарника:** Хороший текст для лендинга/онбординга: объяснять роль кота через эту ситуацию («когда кто-то рядом, начать проще»).


### Партнёр не обязан помогать или делать то же самое — достаточно присутствия  ·  [04:20](https://youtu.be/XLrVdLl5pP4?t=260)
**Мысль:** Эффект body doubling не требует взаимодействия: человек может складывать бельё, пока вы отвечаете на письма. Работает близость как таковая.

> And it's broader than you might think. The other person does not have to help you. They do not even have to do the same task. They can be folding laundry while you're answering emails. They just have to be in proximity. They just have to be there.

### Когда body doubling вредит: зависимость от аудитории, перформанс, стыд  ·  [08:50](https://youtu.be/XLrVdLl5pP4?t=530)
**Мысль:** Исследование 2026 года: у взрослых с СДВГ, учившихся в прямом эфире, эффект со временем пропал: внимание сместилось на выступление, появилась зависимость от стимуляции, стыд и тревога от наблюдения. Сигнал: если поддержка превращается в давление или без неё не работается, менять формат.

> Body doubling does not work for everyone, and it does not always keep working. A 2026 study followed ADHD adults who live-streamed themselves studying, which is body doubling but with an audience. And at first, it worked. Being seen helped them focus, but over time, it didn't. Their attention drifted from actually studying to performing for the audience. They became dependent on the stimulation and could not work without it. And some felt real shame and anxiety about being watched, worried that people would think they were slacking. There is a money side, too. A lot of co-working apps are paid, and workers often end up covering the cost out of their own pocket. So, body doubling is a tool kind of with a double-edged sword, I guess. The moment it tips from support into pressure, or you find you cannot function without it, that is your signal to change the setup rather than push through.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает дизайн Напарника: присутствие без наблюдателя и без оценки, без публичных стримов, лидербордов или социального давления. Следить, чтобы кот не стал костылём: помогать самому начинать.
