A good task management system is another way to externalize your executive function struggles with mental organization of information and memory and all of that,.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник как внешняя память: очередь шагов, видимые часы, «что дальше».


### Разделяй решение и действие по времени или активности  ·  [06:09](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=369)
**Мысль:** Деньги топлива уходят на планирование; если разнести планирование и старт, остаётся энергия активироваться.

> So, when you are trying to decide what to do next, you're decide you're trying to plan, you're trying to figure out what action to take next, and then you're trying to get started on it, sometimes that mental organization that's required in the deciding piece takes enough processing power, takes enough of that, you know, executive function fuel in the executive function fuel tank, so to speak, that you don't have a lot left over to activate. So, one of the very simple things that I encourage my clients to do is to find ways to separate the deciding from the doing. So, separating the deciding, separating the planning, separating the prioritization from actually getting started on the task. So, if you can separate with some span of time or some other kinds of activities, your process for figuring out what you're going to do when in some way from actually getting started on whatever it is that you intend to do, you may have more energy and you may have more focus and you may have more ability to activate and get started on that thing.

**Для Напарника:** Вечерний или утренний «план-на-завтра» отдельно от Фокуса; в Фокус человек приходит с готовым шагом.


### Без плана день диктует то, что попало в поле внимания  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=434)
**Мысль:** Планирование нелюбимо: одни перепланируют, другие избегают; но без него остаётся реактивность и тушение пожаров. Назвать можно как угодно.

> Most of my clients don't love the word planning. Most of us with ADD have sort of a love-hate relationship with planning. Some of us are over-planners, so we burn ourselves up, we use, you know, sort of use all of our energy up with making the perfect plan. Others of us find it really hard to sit down and force ourselves to plan. But, when you're determining what action to take when, that's a form of planning. So, if you can call it whatever you want, I actually call it setting myself up for the next day. I have clients that just call it the deciding, and that's okay, right? But, that form of planning is an important thing for us to do. If we're not planning in some way, again, no matter what you call it, if you're not planning in some way, you're letting whatever comes into your attention direct your action. So, you're reactive, you're your whole day is dictated by email, your whole day is dictated by whatever comes into your attention, or whatever sort of fires need to be put out in the moment. And that's not proactive, that's not moving anything forward, that's just basically treading water and keeping up. And most of us do a lot of that.

**Для Напарника:** Назвать планирование мягко («настроить завтра», «набросок на день»), не «план»; не требовать идеального.


### План не работает без паузы перед стартом: мозгу нужно дозаправиться  ·  [09:02](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=542)
**Мысль:** Планирование и действие берут из одного источника; нужен разрыв по времени или активности, чтобы восстановиться.

> But, that's not enough. It's not enough just to have a plan. We can still have trouble executing the plan or activating or getting started when we need to if we're not careful about when we plan with respect to when we actually intend to get started. So, anytime that you have to make a plan, decide what comes next, it can really be helpful to separate that from the actual getting started by some chunk of time or some other kind of activity that isn't quite so executive function intensive. I hope that makes sense, but got to give your brain a chance to recharge. You've got to give your brain a chance to sort of fuel back up, so to speak. And remember, the planning or the deciding and the getting started or the doing are both taking from the same energy source. So, again, conserve that energy, make it easier for yourself to actually get stuff done,.

**Для Напарника:** После составления шага предлагать маленькую паузу (вдох, вода) перед стартом таймера.


### Не планируй в час упадка и прерываний; подгоняй систему под себя  ·  [10:09](https://youtu.be/Cw2uijd2SOY?t=609)
**Мысль:** Планирование стоит ставить в момент энергии и без помех; чужой способ редко подходит.

> You don't want to slate your planning, you don't want to slate your deciding part for a point in your day when your energy's really low or a point in your day where your focus is really low. You don't want to slate your planning for a time in the day when you're prone to interruptions, right? You want to You want to look at your life, look at how you do things most naturally, how you do things most effectively, what you're already doing now that's working well, and figure out how to make these things work for yourself. You can't try to plug yourself into someone else's way of doing things. It very rarely works.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-ин: «когда у тебя больше сил?» и подгонка напоминаний и Фокуса под ответ.



# 🎬 Finch App Review - Your Digital Best Friend (Mental Wellbeing App) — Holistic Healing Choices


### Микро-цели для тяжёлых дней: малые победы складываются  ·  [02:24](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=144)
**Мысль:** Для плохих дней в Finch есть крошечные задачи (выйти на улицу, встать на 10 секунд, посмотреть в зеркало), чтобы день всё равно засчитался.

> These ones sort of get you to just do the little things. So, little wins add up, which I really am a fan of these apps including in here. There's like things like step outside once, stand up for 10 seconds, look in the mirror and say I can do it, and literally survive the day.

**Для Напарника:** Для Дома: мягкий вариант «сегодня минимум» (один крошечный шаг вместо плана), в голосе напарника, без вины за пустой день.


### Быстрый доступ к SOS-инструментам в момент стресса  ·  [04:41](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=281)
**Мысль:** Кнопка SOS даёт дыхательные техники и приёмы заземления (правило 3-3-3, 5-4-3-2-1) без поиска по меню.

> Now, here you've got all your stuff that is a quick access guide to. So, if you're feeling stressed, you can quickly hit the SOS, and you've got breathing techniques, anxiety breathing, regroup time, and a whole bunch of stuff just to help you in that moment.

**Для Напарника:** На Доме возможна тихая «Мне тяжело» реплика-кнопка с короткой дыхательной паузой голосом напарника; без toast.


### Награда за базовые вещи отвлекает фокус от проблем  ·  [12:26](https://youtu.be/7opbdtb9XgQ?t=746)
**Мысль:** Приложение помогает людям с тревогой: мягкое внимание на питомце и награда за простое (встать с кровати, меньше TikTok) смещают фокус.

> I personally believe that it would be helpful for the right people because it would take their focus away from some of their issues then and there while you're sending this little guy on an adventure as well as giving you those little basic things that you might miss as well as some that are very important like expressing gratitude towards people who you love, getting organizing and stuff with your friends and just that sort of stuff in general. And you can add as much as you like or as little as you like. And I think the quality of what they are, so using less YouTube, using less TikTok, being less distracted, focusing on this, at least just getting out of bed or literally just surviving the day, you get rewarded for.

**Для Напарника:** Награждать за малое (начал шаг, вернулся) в голосе кота, не только за результат.



# 🎬 Finch Self-care app. How I use it and how it has helped me. — Jeena's wellness journey and tips


### Фокус-таймер для слепоты времени, гиперфокуса и перфекционизма  ·  [02:16](https://youtu.be/Fv7BhsX5YAQ?t=136)
**Мысль:** Таймер помогает людям с СДВГ не застревать на одном деле и знать, когда «достаточно хорошо»; помодоро работает для многих.

> Timers. If you also have issues with time blindness or have ADHD and tend to hyperfocus and forget be forgetful, you know, or spend too much time being a perfectionist on one thing when really it's best for you to, you know, learn how to move on when good enough is good enough. So there's a focus timer. That way you use your time better and not just like get stuck on just one thing and know when to move.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает ценность сессий Фокуса; мягкий выход «хватит, достаточно» в голосе кота вместо давления продолжать.


### Категории готовых целей и «лёгкая победа»; вещи рядом, чтобы старт был простым  ·  [04:44](https://youtu.be/Fv7BhsX5YAQ?t=284)
**Мысль:** Когда перегружен, готовые подсказки по категориям (лёгкие победы, гигиена, сон) снимают выбор; цель вроде «выпить воду/лекарство» даёт ощущение достижения за 2 минуты; предметы рядом упрощают старт.

> You're like, okay, like I'm overwhelmed. I have so many things I wish was I could improve about myself or do, right? Well, there's suggestions, right? There's things like, if you look on the bottom of the screen, there's goals categorized by easy wins, hygiene, sleep, movement, gratitude, nutrition, self-kindness, connection, productivity, calm. So, and they're all really good suggestions. for example, if you're someone who struggles a lot with like depression, or if you have chronic illnesses, it's hard to get out of bed. You know, it's easy to be very down on yourself. So, I like to do an easy win, which is I'll literally just make a goal that says took my morning medication or, you know, drink water. Like that's still a really good goal to have. So, all the automatically as soon as you get up, you know, you already feel like more accomplished, right? And that only took you like what, like 2 minutes if that if you keep the water by your bed and also your medication. That kind of helps me with ADHD. like if I need to use something, it needs to be close to where I am essentially, you know, all together in one spot.

**Для Напарника:** На Фокусе и Доме предлагать готовые микрошаги вместо пустого поля; идея «положи нужное рядом» как подсказка для запуска задачи.


### Чем конкретнее цель, тем выше вероятность её выполнить  ·  [12:52](https://youtu.be/Fv7BhsX5YAQ?t=772)
**Мысль:** Конкретика (пять минут прогулки после чистки зубов) привязывает действие к триггеру и снижает размытость.

> Walk around my neighborhood for 5 minutes after I brush my teeth. The more specific you can get with a goal, the more likely you're able to do it.

**Для Напарника:** Дробление задачи (api/breakdown) должно давать конкретные микрошаги с триггером и временем.


### Заменяй плохую привычку другой, иначе остаётся «пустота»  ·  [13:46](https://youtu.be/Fv7BhsX5YAQ?t=826)
**Мысль:** Если убрать привычку без замены, образуется пробел; вместо доомскроллинга перед сном выбрано слушать успокаивающую музыку.

> And if you're going to stop a bad habit, you need to be able to replace it with something because now you have a gap, you know, a gap of nothingness, right? So, instead of doom scrolling, I picked listen to soothing music before bed.

**Для Напарника:** Вместо «не открывай соцсети» предлагать мягкую замену (послушать костёр с котом, звуки природы).



# 🎬 Graphic Design Principles Master-Class! (Ep 1 White Space) — Satori Graphics


### Белое пространство активно: паника перед пустотой, а воздух успокаивает  ·  [00:32](https://youtu.be/FDFD2-poyGw?t=32)
**Мысль:** Новички заполняют пустоту, но добавление отступов делает дизайн спокойнее и увереннее без новых элементов.

> White space isn't empty. It's actually an important and somewhat active part of a design. A huge problem early on is that new designers often panic when they see blank space on their designs. They feel the urge to fill it with something or anything. And let's face it, some beginner clients do this, too. But try this. Take a busy flyer or poster and then add generous padding around the text blocks, space between the sections, and breathing room around the logo. Suddenly, everything calms down. Your eyes feel like they can finally relax when looking at this design. This is your canvas learning how to breathe. It creates balance, structure, and confidence. And without even adding a new element, the design looks 10 times better.

**Для Напарника:** Оставлять воздух на Доме и Фокусе; пустота это не минус, а ноль, и совпадает с тоном продукта.



# 🎬 Here's What Makes Incremental Games FUN! — Developing Ideal Idle Incremental Game


### Оффлайн-доход: прогресс идёт, пока тебя нет  ·  [08:58](https://youtu.be/hty_r4LIRb0?t=538)
**Мысль:** Сохраняется время выхода, а при возвращении секунды умножаются на скорость производства, так игра награждает за возвращение.

> Another important aspect of virtually every incremental game is offline earnings so let's add them to the DU game the simpler the game the easier is to add the offline earnings mechanic in our case it will be a breeze we just save the time when the player close the game and count how many seconds they were offline once they start the game again then we can multiply these seconds by the duck production value and receive the amount of ducks produced when the player was offline.

**Для Напарника:** Остров продолжает «жить» между сессиями (небо меняется, существо у очага), и возвращение встречает приятным сюрпризом без вины.



# 🎬 How AI Helps My ADHD Brain — Aaron Newcomer


### ИИ убирает чистый лист: реагировать на черновик легче, чем создавать с нуля  ·  [02:46](https://youtu.be/ZssxnYgc2wI?t=166)
**Мысль:** Старт из пустоты парализует; полу-мысль, возвращённая ИИ в виде варианта, даёт за что зацепиться и что поправить.

> The single hardest thing for an ADHD brain to do is usually starting something from nothing, a blank page. It's just intimidating. For a lot of us, it's genuinely paralyzing, and there's no edge to grab onto, no momentum to borrow. It's just white space and the sound of your own brain refusing to capitulate. Or, there's white space, and you fill it up for 12 hours, and then you drop it. So, what does AI do? And this sounds almost too simple. It eliminates that blank page. I don't have to generate from nothing anymore. I give Claude a messy half thought, and it gives me something back. I'm really good at generating messy half thoughts. And it's something that I can react to, something I can push back on, something I can say no, not like that, more like this, and I'm I'm reacting to something. That my brain can do all day.

**Для Напарника:** Чат напарника отвечает на сырую мысль готовым первым шагом, который можно отклонить («не так, а вот так»), а не просит сформулировать задачу идеально.


### Переключение контекста: выгрузи состояние перед паузой, верни себя за 30 секунд  ·  [04:04](https://youtu.be/ZssxnYgc2wI?t=244)
**Мысль:** После перерыва теряется не работа, а ментальное состояние; запись «где я, что дальше» в диалог позволяет мгновенно вернуться.

> Here's one that I don't hear people talk about enough, context switching. If you have ADHD, you know what I mean. You're working on something, you get interrupted by my kid, a notification, a completely unrelated thought that suddenly feels very urgent. And when you come back to what you were doing, it's just gone. Not the work. The work is still there, but the mental state you were in to do the work. I mean, it's not uncommon for me to drive all the way to a store to pick one thing up, get to the store, and completely forget the thing that I just went to the store for. That is genuinely the way my brain can dysfunction, but it also happens at work. And what I do now, and this has genuinely changed how I work, is I keep a running Claude conversation for anything I'm working on over multiple sessions. Before I stop, I dump everything into it, where I am, what I was thinking, what the next problem is, what I was about to do before I got interrupted. And when I come back, I can simply ask Claude to catch me back up to where we left off, even if it's 2 days later, even if it's a month later. Where were we? 30 seconds later, I'm back.

**Для Напарника:** Перед выходом из Фокуса напарник спрашивает «на чём остановились?» и при возвращении кратко напоминает: «ты остановилась на…».


### Паралич решений: ИИ даёт черновик, человек правит  ·  [05:20](https://youtu.be/ZssxnYgc2wI?t=320)
**Мысль:** Десять решений до старта выключают мозг; готовый вариант, который можно поменять, сокращает путь с 45 минут до 30 секунд.

> Another thing, ADHD and decision paralysis are best friends. Too many options, brain shuts down. Or, too many options, brain goes into open-ended questions with no constraint, brain shuts down. Task that requires 10 decisions before you can even start the actual task, brain shuts down. I used to do this thing where I'd sit down to write an email, like a simple professional email that should take 30 seconds to respond to, and I'd spend 45 minutes staring at it because I couldn't figure out the right tone, the right length, correct punctuation, the right way to sign off. I don't know how to say this thing. How can I even start an email, a Slack message, a way to respond to just say thank you where thank you would have been sufficient, but then I'm going to go over 47 options about how I should actually write this really simple response. Now, I can just ask Claude to read it over if I'm worried that it's going to come across the right way, or I can ask it to draft up an email that I can change to my personal brand, and then ask if it's okay. 30 seconds, I have a draft. Change some words, change the way that it sounds a little bit, ask Claude if it's still okay for the context that I'm in. Boom, email sent.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник предлагает один вариант первого шага и тёплый выбор «так / по-другому», вместо открытых вопросов.


### Ночные мысли: принимай выгрузку в любое время, без суда и объяснений  ·  [06:35](https://youtu.be/ZssxnYgc2wI?t=395)
**Мысль:** Ясные мысли приходят в неудобное время и испаряются; безоценочный приёмник даёт место, куда вернуться.

> You know how your brain works really well at completely inconvenient times, like 2:00 a.m. or the right before you're supposed to be somewhere, in the shower maybe, when you have no way to write anything down? For years, I would have these moments of clarity where I'd figure out all of the world's problems that I'd been stuck on for a week. And by the time I sat down at my desk the next morning, they were just gone, evaporated into the ether. And now I have a standing instruction in my Claude setup. If I come in with a brain dump at any hour, just receive it, organize it, and hold it for me. No judgment. It's just an AI, so there's no judgment. no need to explain, no need to push back, just catch what I throw.

**Для Напарника:** Быстрое «выгрузить мысль» в чате в любой час; напарник принимает, раскладывает и хранит без комментариев.


### ИИ не лечит СДВГ, он снижает трение в точках, где мозг ломается  ·  [07:19](https://youtu.be/ZssxnYgc2wI?t=439)
**Мысль:** Честная рамка: стоимость потери контекста, старта и избытка решений падает, но мозг не перепрошивается.

> I want to be honest about something though. AI doesn't fix ADHD. I still lose things. I still hyperfocus on the wrong time. I still sometimes spend 2 hours on the part of a project that needed 20 minutes or 30 seconds, and avoid the parts that need 2 hours. I have 47 different novel ideas in Claude right now because, you know, ADHD things. What it changed is the cost of those things. When I lose context, I can get back faster. When I can't start, I have something to react to. When I have too many decisions or too much research to do, I have something to narrow them down in time. It didn't rewire my brain. It just reduced the friction at exactly the points where my brain creates the most.

**Для Напарника:** Честная формулировка в тексте продукта: «Напарник не лечит, он снижает трение»; не обещать излечения.



# 🎬 How Do You Design Accessible Filters for Cognitive Disabilities? - Web Accessibility For All — Ariel Ferro


### Авто-применение без обратной связи путает когнитивно уязвимых  ·  [05:12](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=312)
**Мысль:** Люди с когнитивными задержками не замечают, что страница изменилась; лучше явная кнопка применить или чёткий отклик и перенос фокуса.

> Pitfall 2, filters auto applying with no feedback: Ah, yes! The magic interface where filters apply automagically without hitting a button. It's sleek, right? But here is what goes wrong: users with cognitive delays might not realize the page just changed. Screen reader users may not know results updated, because there's no focus shift. If you're going to auto apply, give clear feedback; announce changes with polite area leave regions. Move focus to the results heading. Or better yet! Let users apply filters manually, so they are always in control.

**Для Напарника:** Если в Мире/Доме появятся фильтры, применять их явным действием и озвучивать результат котом.


### Сброс должен быть заметен для людей с проблемами рабочей памяти  ·  [06:24](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=384)
**Мысль:** Слабо заметная кнопка «Очистить» — кошмар для тех, кто не помнит, что выбрал и как начать заново.

> Pitfall 3, unclear reset functionality: How often do you see a Clear Filters button that is barely visible? For users with executive function difficulties, or short-term memory challenges, this is a nightmare! They can't remember what they chose and can't figure out how to start over. Always offer a Reset or Clear All function. Make it visible. Announce it. Clearly.

**Для Напарника:** На экране Фокуса дать понятный «начать заново/сменить шаг» без наказания.


### Показывать активные настройки простым текстом и давать откат  ·  [07:35](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=455)
**Мысль:** Невидимые состояния по умолчанию превращают фильтр в судоку; активные значения нужно показывать явно и легко отменять.

> Problem one filtering is often a puzzle not a tool filtering interfaces can feel like sudoku especially for users with cognitive or executive function disabilities too many choices filters that interact in weird ways or unclear default states turn what should be a helpful refinement tool into a wall of options design tip make defaults visible show active filters in plain text and always give users an easy way to backtrack.

**Для Напарника:** Выбранную длительность/режим на экране Фокуса показывать текстом, с лёгким возвратом назад.


### Невидимое трение: неопределённость = стресс или shutdown  ·  [10:44](https://youtu.be/9E6jKXq97mY?t=644)
**Мысль:** Каждая двусмысленная подпись и непредсказуемый результат — налог на усталый мозг; помогает эхо действия («показано 24 результата») и отмена.

> Cognitive challenges: Now let's talk about invisible friction. if you are neurodivergent, have brain fog, or experience cognitive fatigue from chronic illness, then every ambiguous label, every unpredictable result, every tiny font rapid visual shift, takes a toll. Examples: sort dropdowns that reset on every new search. Filter options that don't match the terms in the data. Interfaces where nothing tells you how many results your search returned, so you don't know if it is working or not. For some people, the ambiguity causes stress. For others it causes shutdown. Either way it means loss of access. Reduce ambiguity. echo back the user action. -showing 24 results for hiking boots size10. Let users Undo a sort or filter without starting over. And test your interface with real users who think differently.

**Для Напарника:** Эхо действия голосом кота («Записал: шаг «X»») и возможность отмены без потери прогресса.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Built a $1B App Empire (And You Can Too) — Anything 


### Стрик меряет «пришёл», а не мастерство; нужна защита от срыва  ·  [10:16](https://youtu.be/i9S0pp97fEw?t=616)
**Мысль:** Измерять появление, а не результат, делает ежедневное участие достижимым; но нужны механики защиты, чтобы один занятой день не убил серию и не заставил бросить.

> Because a streak redefines progress. Instead of measuring mastery, which is slow and discouraging, a streak measures showing up. That shift makes daily engagement feel achievable. If your app has any daily use case, fitness, journaling, learning, habit tracking, meditation, build a streak mechanic. But a simple day counter isn't enough. You need protection mechanics so users don't lose a 60-day streak to a busy Tuesday and quit forever.

**Для Напарника:** Метрика «пришёл посидеть с котом» вместо результата; пропуск не обнуляет остров, а прощается.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Engineered a Billion-Dollar Habit — Company Mechanics


### Стрик работает внутри системы; заморозка спасает привычку  ·  [03:05](https://youtu.be/ayDAXF7dKgY?t=185)
**Мысль:** Короткие уроки по низкой мотивации, очки, квесты, лидерборды, напоминания; заморозка нужна, чтобы одна ошибка не стерла двухлетнюю серию и не вызвала уход.

> But the streak would not be powerful on its own. It works because Duolingo surrounds it with a behavioral system. Lessons are short enough to finish when motivation is low. Experience points turn practice into a score. Daily quests create immediate targets. Leaderboards convert private progress into competition. Notifications arrive when the streak is at risk. And Streak Freeze protects users from the moment that could destroy the habit completely. If one mistake erased a two-year streak, the frustration could make someone abandon the app. A freeze preserves the emotional investment and gives the user a reason to return.

**Для Напарника:** Короткие сессии для низкой энергии (5-10 мин) и мягкая «заморозка» для пропуска.



# 🎬 How Duolingo Turned a Free Language App Into a $7.7B Business | WSJ The Economics Of — The Wall Street Journal


### Люди готовы практиковаться с ИИ, потому что он не осуждает  ·  [06:48](https://youtu.be/9KqrnBiyBQ8?t=408)
**Мысль:** Люди говорят «да» человеку-партнёру, но не хотят делать это прямо сейчас; ИИ снимает страх оценки, даже если оценивает.

> So you're telling me that if you could, on Duolingo, press a button and you could have another human on the other side, be able to practice, you would use it?" And they'd say, "Sure, I would use it." We even asked, "Would you pay for it?" Like, "Oh, sure, I would pay for it." And then came the very revealing question. We'd say, "Okay, do you want to do it right now?" 100% of people would say, "No, no, no, not right now." What I love about AI is that people don't feel like the AI is judging them, even though it actually is judging them.

**Для Напарника:** ИИ-напарник как безопасное пространство без стыда для начала задачи; подчёркивать «здесь тебя не оценивают».



# 🎬 How Duolingo Turns Your Streak Into Money — Brand Casefile


### A/B Duolingo: стрик по одному уроку вместо дневной цели, +3.3% активных через 14 дней  ·  [02:13](https://youtu.be/415kRd__tQM?t=133)
**Мысль:** Отделив сохранение стрика от выполнения дневной цели, Duolingo упростил минимум на сегодня, и доля вернувшихся через 14 дней выросла на 3.3%. Снижение планки удерживает привычку.

> How much effort should keeping that relationship alive require? In an experiment described in 2020, Dolingo separated the streak from the learner's daily points goal. Completing one lesson could preserve the streak even if the larger goal remained unfinished. The company reported a 3.3% relative increase in the share of learners still active 14 days later. That was an improvement in returning users, not a measured increase in revenue. Look closely at the decision. Dolingo made it easier to do less today and more people were still around 2 weeks later. For someone exhausted before bed, completing a daily goal sounds like another obligation. Finishing one lesson sounds manageable. Sometimes keeping a habit alive means lowering the hurdle.

**Для Напарника:** Минимальная засчитываемая единица в Напарнике должна быть крошечной (одна короткая сессия или даже один шаг). «Плана нет — это ноль, не минус»: день с малым действием остаётся живым.



# 🎬 How Gameplay Loops Keep You Playing — Adam Millard - The Architect of Games


### Свобода без структуры приводит к скуке и растерянности  ·  [06:41](https://youtu.be/Sk-nbAtIUko?t=401)
**Мысль:** Полностью открытый мир теряет игроков; разбивка на восемь залов и синдикаты даёт посильные шаги к большой цели.

> As much as people claim to want total freedom, the sheer directionlessness of a hypothetical open world pokemon game for example would more often than not lead to people getting lost, confused and critically bored before they’d completed the game. But, by splitting things up into 8 gym leaders and one or two crime syndicates, each containing a number of goes around the core battling loop, devs can ensure that their players don’t feel overwhelmed as they slowly progress towards completing the next big story loop,.

**Для Напарника:** Не давать пустой экран свободного выбора: предлагать один следующий шаг (очередь), дробя большую цель.



# 🎬 How Games Use Feedback Loops — Game Maker's Toolkit


### В Pyre проигрыш всё равно даёт опыт, нет перманентных потерь: ошибка — кочка, не точка невозврата  ·  [06:44](https://youtu.be/H4kbJObhcHw?t=404)
**Мысль:** Положительная петля без компенсатора ведёт к спирали неудач; парой с отрицательной петлёй (опыт за поражение, нет permadeath) игра остаётся в зоне вызова.

> But Pyre counteracts this issue, by pairing those positive feedback loops - with negative feedback loops. So, yes, losing matches sucks but - here’s loop number one - you still receive some experience points for failing. Meaning that you’re not punished too much for messing up. Also, unlike XCOM, there’s no permadeath so you’re not massively screwed for future matches. And then on the other side of things, counteracting the issue of overpowered troops, there’s the liberation system. You see, Pyre is set in a sort of grim purgatory called the Downside - and the entire point of the game is to win these special liberation rites - because the prize is that the team’s leader gets to leave the Downside, have their crimes forgotten, and return to the Commonwealth. And in the game, this means that at various intervals throughout the adventure, you permanently send away your most powerful soldiers, because you can only liberate those who are of a high enough rank So I guess Pyre does have permadeath... but it’s a good thing. And all this means that the resulting experience - at least for me - is that the game is always a close and interesting challenge. Mistakes became a road bump on the way to the conclusion, not a tipping point that could send me into an inescapable death spiral.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевой принцип проекта: сессия, которую бросили или недоделали, всё равно что-то даёт (опыт, тёплая реплика), а не обнуляет прогресс.



# 🎬 How I Fixed a Client's Onboarding and Won the Contract — Bear Liu


### Не просить запоминать и не объяснять интерфейс интерфейсом  ·  [08:33](https://youtu.be/S7eGDOkd_Pg?t=513)
**Мысль:** Ментальная энергия ограничена: чем проще, тем лучше. Если интерфейс настолько сложен, что для объяснения нужен ещё слой (тултипы, карусели), надо упростить первый слой, а не добавлять второй.

> And another typical onboarding pitfall we have, besides being all about features, is also something around asking people to memorize something, and this is also not recommended because — I don't say people are lazy, just people have limited mental load, right, so we have very short attention spans and we have very limited time and effort to remember or to think about something. We want to save that energy, save the energy for the very important things. So the simpler, the easier it is for people, the better the results will be, right. And also the worst thing about the onboarding experience is to use an interface to explain another interface. This happened to me a while ago when I just started doing UI design and I thought it was really cool — it seemed like every software app used tooltips, they used something like this to introduce, oh, this is something you can do, and this is a carousel people can swipe to see different things. But this is definitely not what we want to do. If your interface is so complicated that you need to use another interface to explain it, you probably want to improve the first layer interface and not add another layer.

**Для Напарника:** Внутренние экраны Напарника должны быть понятны без подсказок: одна цель на экран, короткие подписи. Для СДВГ-аудитории нагрузка на рабочую память особенно критична.



# 🎬 How Idle Games Infected The Entire Games Industry — Shoobaloo


### В реальной жизни прогресс невидим, поэтому тянет к играм с видимым ростом  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=66)
**Мысль:** Мы часто не получаем награды за домашние дела и работу в короткой перспективе, и прогресс кажется отсутствующим. Игры, где число растёт, дают то, чего не хватает в жизни.

> As you go through life, do you ever question whether you're making progress? Sometimes it's difficult to tell because you often aren't rewarded for things in the short term or even at all. When was the last time you received a tangible reward for doing the dishes or cleaning your room? Does your mommy still fork up for your allowance? My wife gives me mine on Tuesdays, I was just curious. Progress can sometimes feel invisible or even non-existent in real life.

**Для Напарника:** Ключевая ценность Напарника: сделать невидимый прогресс (маленькие шаги по делам) видимым через остров и кота.


### Единственное правило: число растёт, прогресс certain и виден  ·  [15:10](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=910)
**Мысль:** Число символизирует прогресс; игры делают прогресс гарантированным и видимым, чего не делает реальная жизнь.

> You see, all these incremental games essentially function the same. The one and only rule of an incremental game is the number must go up. That number, regardless of how it's presented on screen, is a representation of progress. These games make progress certain and visible. When's the last time real life did that for you?

**Для Напарника:** Гарантия видимого прироста за любую завершённую минуту фокуса (ноль не минус).


### Игры дают структуру: понятные правила, усилие равно награде, прогресс неизбежен  ·  [17:20](https://youtu.be/ZjrVc_5QNKY?t=1040)
**Мысль:** Реальная жизнь не гарантирует прогресса, а идле-игры гарантируют. Структура, где правила осмысленны, а усилие вознаграждается, - то, чего людям не хватает.

> Real life will always be messy, unpredictable, and full of friction that we can't just automate away with a hive mind of cookie-baking grandmas that bring about the grandmacalypse. Real life doesn't guarantee progress, but these boring games do. And that is exactly why they are so magnetic, so addictive. They provide a kind of structure where the rules make sense, where effort equals bountiful reward, and progress is imminent. I think that is truly the problem that these games solve.

**Для Напарника:** Внешняя структура для людей с СДВГ: ясные правила, что считается прогрессом, и надёжное вознаграждение за усилие.



# 🎬 How To 4X Your Shopify Conversion Rate (Complete CRO Guide) — Davie Fogarty Mentors


### Путаный интерфейс заставляет человека «сжигать калории» на мысли  ·  [13:12](https://youtu.be/A-7pTamjBIw?t=792)

If it's clunky and confusing, what's going to happen is the customers is actually going to burn calories trying to think. And people don't want to burn calories thinking about stuff. Our bodies naturally try to prevent ourselves from doing that.
